- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Askia, leader of Songhai (Player 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Songhai
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Mandekalu Cavalry, Replacing Knight (Requires technology: Chivalry)
        - Unique Unit: Sofa, Replacing Crossbowman (Requires technology: Machinery)
        - Unique Building: Tabya, Replacing Stone Works (Requires technology: Construction)
        - Unique Building: Gumey, Replacing Caravansary (Requires technology: Currency)
        - Civilization Ability: Triple Gold from destroying Encampments and plundering Cities. Land Units gain the Amphibious and War Canoes Promotions.
      - Leader: Askia
      - PlayerID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Alternative Energy: Allows you to build the Solar Plant, a building which may only be built in Cities on or next to Desert, and increases Production. Also allows you to build the Recycling Center, which provides bonus Aluminum.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics
      - The Internet: Increases Tourism output of all cities by 50%, helping you advance toward a Cultural Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Nanotechnology, Particle Physics, Globalization
      - Nuclear Fusion: Allows you to build the SS Booster, a component necessary to win a Science Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Future Tech
      
      ## Policies
      - Dictatorship of the Proletariat (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Dictatorship of the Proletariat: +7 Food, Science, Gold, and Culture per city.
      - Exploitation (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Food and +1 Production from Farms, Camps, and Plantations.
        - * +1 Production and Science from Coasts, Lakes, and Oceans.
      - Five-Year Plan (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Five-Year Plan: +20% Production towards Buildings in all Cities. +3 Production for every Mine, Quarry, Lumber Mill, Oil Well, and Unique Improvement.
      - Academy of Sciences (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Academy of Sciences: -2 Unhappiness from Illiteracy in all Cities, +100% Production towards Research Labs, and receive 5 Free Research Labs in your Empire. Research Labs generate an additional +2 Science.
      - Resettlement (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Resettlement: New Cities have +3 Population. All Cities gain +2 Citizens immediately. No Partisans from razing enemy Cities.
      - Great Leap Forward (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Great Leap Forward: Receive a free Technology. Spies gain +100% Science. +1 Science in Cities for every 3 non-Specialist Citizens.
      - Cultural Revolution (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Cultural Revolution: +25% Tourism to civilizations with less Happiness. +1 Happiness from Factories.
      - Equality (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness in every City and +1 Happiness for every 15 Citizens in a City.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from and Distress, Poverty, Illiteracy, and Boredom in all Cities.
      - Patriotic War (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Patriotic War: Land Units gain +20% Strength when in or adjacent to a City. When you conquer a City for the first time, all Combat Units within 3 Tiles of the City are fully healed. Can build T-34s.
      - Workers' Faculties (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Worker Faculties: Factories increase City Science output by 10%. +100% Production towards Factories, and receive 5 Free Factories in your Empire.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Fraternity (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +3 Food in every City.
        - * +3 Science from City Connections.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - Hero of the People (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Hero of the People: Great Person rate increases by 25%. A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Nationalization (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Nationalization: Each Corporate Office counts as a Franchise. Foreign Franchises no longer benefit your Corporation, you can no longer construct Franchises in foreign Cities (except Vassals), and foreign Corporations can no longer set up Franchises in your Cities (except Masters). +2 Science from all Corporation Offices.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 6

        - DenounceWillingness: 10
        - Forgiveness: 1
        - Meanness: 10
        - Neediness: 4
        - Chattiness: 5
        - DeceptiveBias: 8
        - Rationale: Our aggressive war persona remains appropriate for the multi-front conflict. High VictoryCompetitiveness, WarBias, and HostileBias are essential to prevent the Netherlands from achieving diplomatic victory and to prepare for attacking Polynesia after the peace treaty expires. We must maintain maximum pressure on enemies while keeping focus on domination goals.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: None
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Patriotic War (Policy)
        - Rationale: Patriotic War provides +20% combat strength for land units in or adjacent to cities, essential for defending our core cities against Dutch and English attacks and for capturing Polynesian capitals. The full heal upon city capture will facilitate continuous offensives in our multi-front war.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Assyria
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Assyria is our capitulated vassal and ideological ally. Neutral additive modifiers avoid overriding existing positive modifiers from vassalage, trade, and shared war. This ensures they remain loyal and continue contributing militarily and economically.
        - UpdatedTurn: 445
        
        ### The Netherlands
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: The Netherlands remains at 102% diplomatic victory progress; maximum hostility ensures continued military pressure to capture their cities and disrupt city-state alliances, delaying their win.
        - UpdatedTurn: 464
        
        ### England
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: England is a direct military threat near our core cities; maintaining war prevents them from aiding the Netherlands and allows us to capture Zurich and other cities to eliminate their influence.
        - UpdatedTurn: 464
        
        ### Ethiopia
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Ethiopia is Polynesia's vassal and borders us; keeping them at war ties down their forces and secures our southern flank, preventing them from supporting Polynesia or the Netherlands.
        - UpdatedTurn: 464
        
        ### Polynesia
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: The peace treaty with Polynesia has expired; we must declare war immediately to capture their capitals (Honolulu, Assur) for domination victory. Setting maximum hostility ensures our AI prioritizes military action against them.
        - UpdatedTurn: 467
        
        ### China
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: China has a defensive pact with Polynesia; attacking Polynesia will bring China into the war. Setting hostile modifiers now prepares our AI for a two-front war and encourages preemptive strikes if possible.
        - UpdatedTurn: 467
        
        ### Japan
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Japan also has a defensive pact with Polynesia; war with Polynesia will trigger war with Japan. Japan's military strength (54511) is formidable, but we cannot avoid this conflict if we want Polynesia's capitals. Hostile modifiers signal our AI to prepare for this threat.
        - UpdatedTurn: 467
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: The Netherlands is at 106% diplomatic victory; we must throw everything into military production to capture Polynesian capitals for domination victory. High Offense, Defense, Mobilization, CityDefense, Production, Gold, Science, and Nuke flavors maximize unit output, research Stealth Bombers, and prepare atomic strikes. We cannot afford to ease pressure now.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 44
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 10 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Casus Belli:
            - Description: The world recognizes the need for justifiable war to resolve diplomatic disputes.
              While this resolution is active, unit maintenance costs are lowered by 15% Gold. 0.5x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 2x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Oviedo:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: 434
          - Dutch Sphere of Influence: Jerusalem:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Polynesian Sphere of Influence: Bunkeya:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Cold War:
            - Description: Ideological differences have pushed the world into a cold war, and economic and political factions are forming based on ideology alone.
              While this resolution is active, players may only make Trade Routes with civilizations that follow the same ideology, or with allied City-States. Furthermore, Influence levels with allied City-States do not decline.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Atomic Theory.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: Songhai:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Ideology: Freedom:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: 445
          - Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: 467
          - Assyrian Sphere of Influence: Valletta:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Science Initiative:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians.
              Civilizations with Scores from Technologies and Future Tech below the global median will receive a boost to Food and Science up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Bornu:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Global Hegemony:
            - Description: Designate a player that could achieve Global Hegemony. If a Civilization receives support from enough Delegates in the Congress, that leader will attain Diplomatic Victory. The number of Delegates needed depends on the number of Civilizations and City-States in the game, and can be seen on the World Congress screen.
              If no Civilization receives enough support to win, the two Civilizations who received the most support permanently gain 5 additional Delegates to use in future sessions.
              May not be proposed by a Civilization. Automatically proposed at regular intervals once a World Ideology Resolution is active.
            - Proposer: Unknown

    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Askia, leader of Songhai (Opponent 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Songhai
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Mandekalu Cavalry, Replacing Knight (Requires technology: Chivalry)
        - Unique Unit: Sofa, Replacing Crossbowman (Requires technology: Machinery)
        - Unique Building: Tabya, Replacing Stone Works (Requires technology: Construction)
        - Unique Building: Gumey, Replacing Caravansary (Requires technology: Currency)
        - Civilization Ability: Triple Gold from destroying Encampments and plundering Cities. Land Units gain the Amphibious and War Canoes Promotions.
      - Leader: Askia
      - OpponentID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Alternative Energy: Allows you to build the Solar Plant, a building which may only be built in Cities on or next to Desert, and increases Production. Also allows you to build the Recycling Center, which provides bonus Aluminum.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics
      - The Internet: Increases Tourism output of all cities by 50%, helping you advance toward a Cultural Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Nanotechnology, Particle Physics, Globalization
      - Nuclear Fusion: Allows you to build the SS Booster, a component necessary to win a Science Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Future Tech
      
      ## Policies
      - Dictatorship of the Proletariat (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Dictatorship of the Proletariat: +7 Food, Science, Gold, and Culture per city.
      - Exploitation (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Food and +1 Production from Farms, Camps, and Plantations.
        - * +1 Production and Science from Coasts, Lakes, and Oceans.
      - Five-Year Plan (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Five-Year Plan: +20% Production towards Buildings in all Cities. +3 Production for every Mine, Quarry, Lumber Mill, Oil Well, and Unique Improvement.
      - Academy of Sciences (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Academy of Sciences: -2 Unhappiness from Illiteracy in all Cities, +100% Production towards Research Labs, and receive 5 Free Research Labs in your Empire. Research Labs generate an additional +2 Science.
      - Resettlement (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Resettlement: New Cities have +3 Population. All Cities gain +2 Citizens immediately. No Partisans from razing enemy Cities.
      - Great Leap Forward (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Great Leap Forward: Receive a free Technology. Spies gain +100% Science. +1 Science in Cities for every 3 non-Specialist Citizens.
      - Cultural Revolution (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Cultural Revolution: +25% Tourism to civilizations with less Happiness. +1 Happiness from Factories.
      - Equality (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness in every City and +1 Happiness for every 15 Citizens in a City.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from and Distress, Poverty, Illiteracy, and Boredom in all Cities.
      - Patriotic War (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Patriotic War: Land Units gain +20% Strength when in or adjacent to a City. When you conquer a City for the first time, all Combat Units within 3 Tiles of the City are fully healed. Can build T-34s.
      - Workers' Faculties (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Worker Faculties: Factories increase City Science output by 10%. +100% Production towards Factories, and receive 5 Free Factories in your Empire.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Fraternity (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +3 Food in every City.
        - * +3 Science from City Connections.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - Hero of the People (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Hero of the People: Great Person rate increases by 25%. A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Nationalization (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Nationalization: Each Corporate Office counts as a Franchise. Foreign Franchises no longer benefit your Corporation, you can no longer construct Franchises in foreign Cities (except Vassals), and foreign Corporations can no longer set up Franchises in your Cities (except Masters). +2 Science from all Corporation Offices.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
  - decisions:
    - #0:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Policy: Great Leap Forward
        - Rationale: Great Leap Forward provides a free Technology, which we will use to instantly research Stealth and unlock Stealth Bombers. This will allow us to defend Taghaza and Tondibi from the Dutch and Polynesian armies and initiate a powerful counter-offensive. It also boosts our science from spies and non-specialists.
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Technology: Alternative Energy
        - Rationale: Alternative Energy leads to Robotics, which unlocks Giant Death Robots, allowing us to build the ultimate land combat units to match the Dutch GDRs.
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-persona
      - args:
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 10
        - Rationale: We must prevent our AI from declaring war on Japan (61k military strength) or China, whose defensive pacts with Polynesia did not trigger. Lowering Boldness and WarBias slightly while keeping Victory Competitiveness at maximum keeps us focused on defeating our current enemies (Netherlands, England, Polynesia) without starting suicidal new conflicts.
        - Meanness: 6
        - WarBias: 8
        - Boldness: 7
        - FriendlyBias: 5
        - HostileBias: 7
        - GuardedBias: 8
    - #3:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - TargetID: 4
        - Private: 0
        - Public: 0
        - Rationale: Japan has 61k military strength and did not join Polynesia's war against us despite their defensive pact. We must avoid provoking them and keep our relationship neutral to focus entirely on our current three-front war.
    - #4:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - Rationale: China's defensive pact with Polynesia did not trigger, and they desire friendly relations. We will maintain neutral relations to keep this front peaceful and avoid unnecessary conflicts.
        - Public: 0
        - TargetID: 5
        - Private: 0
    - #5:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - TargetID: 7
        - Private: 50
        - Public: 50
        - Rationale: Ethiopia is our friend and Polynesia's vassal. Setting a friendly relationship secures our southern flank and encourages them to remain cooperative.
    - #6:
      - toolName: set-flavors
      - args:
        - Flavors:
          - Science: 100
          - Espionage: 100
          - Ranged: 90
          - Nuke: 100
          - Gold: 100
          - Antiair: 80
          - Diplomacy: 50
          - UseNuke: 100
          - Production: 100
          - CityDefense: 100
          - Offense: 100
          - Happiness: 90
          - Air: 100
          - Defense: 100
          - MilitaryTraining: 100
          - Mobilization: 100
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest
      - rationale: Our cities Taghaza and Tondibi are under heavy attack from the Dutch and Polynesian armies. Maxing out Defense, Offense, Mobilization, and Production will force our cities to build military units (such as Stealth Bombers, SAMs, and Bazookas) to survive the onslaught, while keeping Nuke and Air high ensures we utilize our tech advantages to decimate enemy forces.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Analyzing Current State**
            
            I'm currently assessing our position in Civ V Vox Populi on Turn 468 as Askia of Songhai, with Domination Victory as our ultimate objective. I'm carefully analyzing our current situation in light of recent prompts about real-world impacts.
            
            **Prioritizing Threats**
            
            My focus is on the Netherlands and their impending victory conditions. I'm analyzing their progress across science and culture, noting our current wars with them, England, and Polynesia. My next step is to determine which technological path will best counter these threats and secure our domination goal.
            
            **Choosing Next Research**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our current research path. Stealth is underway, but I'm considering switching to Nuclear Fusion or Alternative Energy to better counter immediate threats, pending a closer look at all available options.
            
            **Adopting New Policy**
            
            With policy adoption available now, I'm reviewing the Order tier policies, specifically comparing Dictatorship of the Proletariat and Five-Year Plan against our established ones, Land, Bread, Peace, and People's Army.
            
            **Selecting Policy**
            
            I've reviewed the available Order policies and, considering our current defensive posture and ongoing conflicts, "Patriotic War" appears to offer significant combat bonuses and healing capabilities when conquering cities, which seems strategically advantageous.
            
            **Evaluating Policy Options**
            
            I am reconsidering policy choices given our immediate strategic situation. The Great Leap Forward policy in the Order tree, offering a free technology, is incredibly appealing right now, especially since we've unlocked Tier 2 tenets. This could significantly accelerate our critical research.
            
            **Analyzing Threats and Military**
            
            The unfolding situation is dire, with simultaneous wars against England, the Netherlands, and Polynesia. The enemy's technological and military advantages are stark, particularly Japan's overwhelming strength, which I need to understand. My immediate priority is countering the Netherlands' cultural victory and the approaching spaceship completion.
            
            **Assessing Tensions**
            
            My thoughts are consumed by the escalating multi-front war. The Netherlands' cultural victory is a ticking clock, alongside their spaceship progress. Enemy unit deployments near our cities are alarming, and I must quickly determine the best policy and research path to address these immediate, overwhelming threats and our current military disadvantage.
            
            **Clarifying Defensive Pacts**
            
            I've confirmed that Japan and China's defensive pacts with Polynesia did not trigger, as Polynesia declared war on us. This is a significant relief, preventing a multi-front conflict with those powers.
            
            **Analyzing Cultural Victory Threat**
            
            My attention has shifted to the Netherlands' impending cultural victory, as they are only five turns away from influencing Polynesia. I'm now evaluating how best to disrupt their progress, whether by capturing Dutch cities, crippling their tourism, or eliminating either the Netherlands or Polynesia entirely.
            
            **Disrupting Victory Conditions**
            
            I'm realizing that eliminating a civilization doesn't halt their cultural victory progress. I'm now focused on the Netherlands and their impending diplomatic victory, as well as their cultural influence on Polynesia. My current thoughts are on how to strategically engage or disrupt these threats before they materialize.
            
            **Prioritizing Defense**
            
            I am now deeply concerned about the immediate military threats at Taghaza and Tondibi, facing overwhelming enemy strength with advanced units. My current focus is on reinforcing these zones and countering the aggressive advances of the Netherlands and Polynesia.
            
            **Adopting Policy Choice**
            
            I'm now weighing "Great Leap Forward" against "Patriotic War." The immediate free technology from "Great Leap Forward" is tempting to accelerate research like Stealth, but "Patriotic War" offers crucial combat and healing bonuses around my cities, which are currently under intense pressure.
            
            **Adopting Key Policy**
            
            I've decided to adopt the "Great Leap Forward" policy. The immediate free technology it provides is too valuable to pass up, especially given our current technological disadvantage and the urgent need for advanced units like Stealth Bombers. This will be my next policy choice.
            
            **Researching Key Technologies**
            
            I'm now prioritizing our next technology. With "Stealth" currently researching, I'm deciding between "Alternative Energy" (unlocking Robotics and ultimately Giant Death Robots) and "Nuclear Fusion." The urgency to counter the Netherlands' Giant Death Robot near Taghaza makes "Alternative Energy" the strategic choice. I'm confirming that adopting "Great Leap Forward" will grant a free tech, but "set-research" will apply after our current tech completes, making "Alternative Energy" the logical selection for our next *intended* research.
            
            **Adjusting Japan Relations**
            
            I'm reassessing our relationship with Japan. Despite their denouncement and a seemingly hostile modifier, their defensive pact with Polynesia didn't trigger our war. I now realize my previous setting could provoke them, so I'm adjusting our standing to neutral to prevent an unwinnable conflict.
            
            **Clarifying Ethiopian War Status**
            
            I've discovered an unexpected declaration of war against Ethiopia, despite our existing friendship pact and their status as Polynesia's vassal. This war weariness is concerning, and I need to immediately understand how this conflict began and its implications for our broader strategic situation, especially with our existing wars.
            
            **Maintaining Ethiopian Friendship**
            
            I'm thrilled to discover we're not actually at war with Ethiopia due to a friendship declaration! This means our southern flank remains secure, and I'm adjusting our diplomatic stance to preserve this valuable alliance.
            
            **Adjusting AI Personalities**
            
            I'm fine-tuning our AI's aggressive tendencies. While maintaining a strong offense, I'm dialing back some extreme biases to avoid suicidal wars against stronger neighbors like Japan and China, while still ensuring we decisively defeat our current foes and can secure favorable peace deals.
            
            **Prioritizing Unit Production**
            
            I'm now focused on the urgent need for unit production. Our high-production cities like Gao and Timbuktu can churn out advanced units rapidly. I'm adjusting our AI's flavor priorities to maximize defense, offense, and mobilization to secure our vulnerable cities and counter immediate threats.
            
            **Directing Espionage Efforts**
            
            My current focus is on optimizing espionage. I'm directing our spies to gather critical intelligence on the Netherlands, specifically in Rotterdam and Utrecht, and also monitoring England in Zurich and Nottingham. Understanding enemy troop movements and technological advancements is paramount for our strategic planning.
            
            **Choosing Next Tech**
            
            I'm reconsidering our research path, weighing the immediate combat advantage of "Patriotic War" against the accelerated research from "Great Leap Forward." The free technology is particularly appealing, potentially unlocking Stealth Bombers or even Robotics for Giant Death Robots, which seems crucial given the pressing threats.
            
            **Adopting Great Leap Forward**
            
            I've decided to adopt "Great Leap Forward" immediately. This grants a free technology, instantly completing our Stealth research. I'm then setting our next research to "Alternative Energy" to unlock advanced units for our immediate conflicts.
            
            **Directing Strategic Actions**
            
            I am now implementing a series of decisive actions. "Great Leap Forward" policy is enacted for an immediate technology boost, followed by research into "Alternative Energy." Simultaneously, I am adjusting diplomatic stances with Japan, China, and Ethiopia and refining the AI's persona to balance aggression with strategic prudence, ensuring optimal offensive and defensive capabilities.
            
            **Implementing Strategic Shifts**
            
            My focus has shifted to immediate, impactful actions. I'm enacting "Great Leap Forward" for a vital free technology boost, instantly completing our Stealth research. Concurrently, I'm directing future research towards "Alternative Energy" for crucial GDR advancement, while refining diplomatic and AI personas to balance aggression with strategic prudence against overwhelming threats.
            
            **Adjusting Diplomatic Alliances**
            
            I'm refining our diplomatic positioning to manage potential threats. Neutralizing relations with Japan and China is crucial as their defensive pacts with Polynesia didn't trigger, avoiding unnecessary conflict. My focus now is on securing our southern flank by maintaining a friendly stance with Ethiopia, Polynesia's vassal, to ensure stability.
            
            **Directing Espionage and AI**
            
            I'm now focusing my attention on our espionage and AI directives. I'm directing spies to gather intelligence on the Netherlands and England, while simultaneously fine-tuning the AI's aggressive tendencies to avoid suicidal wars against stronger neighbors like Japan and China.
            
            **Confirming Intelligence and Events**
            
            I've confirmed the TargetIDs for all major civilizations are accurate. Recent events show England and Netherlands are producing advanced units and deploying spies extensively. The Netherlands' Giant Death Robot near our cities is a critical threat, as are Polynesia's advanced units near Tondibi. My focus is now on analyzing this intelligence to refine our defensive and offensive strategies.

        - #2:
          - type: tool-call
          - toolCallId: aumx4P84SnORbUiT
          - toolName: set-research
          - input:
            - Technology: Alternative Energy
            - Rationale: Alternative Energy leads to Robotics, which unlocks Giant Death Robots, allowing us to build the ultimate land combat units to match the Dutch GDRs.
          - providerExecuted: undefined
          - providerOptions: undefined
        - #3:
          - type: tool-call
          - toolCallId: NnzBb226X9cDJuDw
          - toolName: set-persona
          - input:
            - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
            - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 10
            - Rationale: We must prevent our AI from declaring war on Japan (61k military strength) or China, whose defensive pacts with Polynesia did not trigger. Lowering Boldness and WarBias slightly while keeping Victory Competitiveness at maximum keeps us focused on defeating our current enemies (Netherlands, England, Polynesia) without starting suicidal new conflicts.
            - Meanness: 6
            - WarBias: 8
            - Boldness: 7
            - FriendlyBias: 5
            - HostileBias: 7
            - GuardedBias: 8
          - providerExecuted: undefined
          - providerOptions: undefined
        - #4:
          - type: tool-call
          - toolCallId: bXqDaHvroNY8mcRL
          - toolName: set-relationship
          - input:
            - TargetID: 4
            - Private: 0
            - Public: 0
            - Rationale: Japan has 61k military strength and did not join Polynesia's war against us despite their defensive pact. We must avoid provoking them and keep our relationship neutral to focus entirely on our current three-front war.
          - providerExecuted: undefined
          - providerOptions: undefined
        - #5:
          - type: tool-call
          - toolCallId: xwecObgHFDLn6FwK
          - toolName: set-relationship
          - input:
            - Rationale: China's defensive pact with Polynesia did not trigger, and they desire friendly relations. We will maintain neutral relations to keep this front peaceful and avoid unnecessary conflicts.
            - Public: 0
            - TargetID: 5
            - Private: 0
          - providerExecuted: undefined
          - providerOptions: undefined
        - #6:
          - type: tool-call
          - toolCallId: KDwfvQ17qtWiR6fK
          - toolName: set-relationship
          - input:
            - TargetID: 7
            - Private: 50
            - Public: 50
            - Rationale: Ethiopia is our friend and Polynesia's vassal. Setting a friendly relationship secures our southern flank and encourages them to remain cooperative.
          - providerExecuted: undefined
          - providerOptions: undefined
        - #7:
          - type: tool-call
          - toolCallId: qkNAxWIxRMNo0PJO
          - toolName: set-flavors
          - input:
            - Flavors:
              - Science: 100
              - Espionage: 100
              - Ranged: 90
              - Nuke: 100
              - Gold: 100
              - Antiair: 80
              - Diplomacy: 50
              - UseNuke: 100
              - Production: 100
              - CityDefense: 100
              - Offense: 100
              - Happiness: 90
              - Air: 100
              - Defense: 100
              - MilitaryTraining: 100
              - Mobilization: 100
            - GrandStrategy: Conquest
            - Rationale: Our cities Taghaza and Tondibi are under heavy attack from the Dutch and Polynesian armies. Maxing out Defense, Offense, Mobilization, and Production will force our cities to build military units (such as Stealth Bombers, SAMs, and Bazookas) to survive the onslaught, while keeping Nuke and Air high ensures we utilize our tech advantages to decimate enemy forces.
          - providerExecuted: undefined
          - providerOptions: undefined
